\displaystyle=t^{-{\lambda}_{a}}I_{{\lambda}_{a}}^{+}t^{{\lambda}_{a+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}t^{-{\lambda}_{c-1}}I_{{\lambda}_{c-1}}^{+}t^{{\lambda}_{c}}\times_{K}t^{-{\lambda}_{c}}I_{{\lambda}_{c}}^{+}t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{c}}K

\displaystyle=K_{U_{{\lambda}_{a}}}t^{{\lambda}^{\natural}_{a}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U_{{\lambda}_{c}}}t^{{\lambda}^{\natural}_{c}}K,

where the fifth equality follows from Lemma
1.4
that
I_{{\lambda}_{\tau}}^{-}\subseteq I_{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\tau}}
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
since
{\lambda}^{\natural}_{\bullet}=-{\lambda_{\bullet}}+{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\bullet}
is minuscule. The proof is finished.
∎

Write
\epsilon_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{d})\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n})^{d}
with
\epsilon_{\tau}:=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{\tau}}^{G}
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
. We define
a_{\tau,i}=\epsilon_{\tau}(i)+n{\lambda}_{\tau}(\epsilon_{\tau}(i))
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
. By the definition of
\epsilon_{\tau}=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{\tau}}
(see also Remark
3.4
),
a_{\tau,1}>\cdots>a_{\tau,n}
is the arrangement of the integers
i+n{\lambda}_{\tau}(i)
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
in the decreasing order. Define
w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}=(w_{\tau})_{1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d}\in(\mathfrak{S}_{n})^{d}
such that

(
\ast
)

\displaystyle a_{\tau,i}=\begin{cases}a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(i)}-n{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(i),&\text{ if }1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d-1;\\
a_{1,w_{d}(i)}-n{\lambda}^{\flat}_{d}(i)+m,&\text{ if }\tau=d.\end{cases}

Lemma 3.7
.

We have
\epsilon_{\tau}=\epsilon_{1}w_{1}^{-1}\cdots w_{\tau-1}^{-1}
and hence
{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}=w_{\tau-1}\cdots w_{1}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}({\lambda}^{\natural}_{\tau})
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
.

Proof.

Suppose
2\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
. Then
a_{\tau,i}=a_{\tau-1,w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i)}+n{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau-1}(w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i))
, that is,

\epsilon_{\tau}(i)+n{\lambda}_{\tau}(\epsilon_{\tau}(i))=\epsilon_{\tau-1}(w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i))+n{\lambda}_{\tau-1}(\epsilon_{\tau-1}(w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i)))+n{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau-1}(w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i)),

which means
\epsilon_{\tau}(i)=\epsilon_{\tau-1}(w_{\tau-1}^{-1}(i))
. By induction we have
\epsilon_{\tau}=\epsilon_{1}w_{1}^{-1}\cdots w_{\tau-1}^{-1}
.

∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
and
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
. We have

(1)
w_{\tau}(i)>w_{\tau}(j)
, that is,
a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(i)}<a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(j)}
if and only if
a_{\tau,i}-a_{\tau,j}<n
and
{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(j)-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(i)=1
, in which case
a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(j)}-a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(i)}<n
.

(2)
\ell(w_{\tau})=|\{\alpha\in\Phi;({\lambda}_{\tau})_{\alpha}\geqslant 0,({\lambda}_{\tau}^{\dagger})_{\alpha}<0\}|
.

Proof.

By (
\ast
) we have

(i)

\displaystyle a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(i)}-a_{\tau+1,w_{\tau}(j)}

\displaystyle=a_{\tau,i}-a_{\tau,j}+n({\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(i)-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(j))

\displaystyle=(\epsilon_{\tau}(i)+n{\lambda}_{\tau}(\epsilon_{\tau}(i)))-(\epsilon_{\tau}(j)+n{\lambda}_{\tau}(\epsilon_{\tau}(j))).

Then the first statement follows from that
{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}
is minuscule. For
1\leqslant k\neq l\leqslant n
we ave
{\lambda}_{\alpha_{k,l}}\geqslant 0
if and only if
k+n{\lambda}(k)>l+n{\lambda}(l)
. Then it follows from (i) that the map
\gamma\mapsto\epsilon_{\tau}(\gamma)
gives a bijection between
\Phi^{+}\cap-w_{\tau}^{-1}(\Phi^{+})
and the set
\{\alpha\in\Phi;({\lambda}_{\tau})_{\alpha}\geqslant 0,({\lambda}_{\tau}^{\dagger})_{\alpha}<0\}
. The second statement is proved.
∎

For
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
we denote by
\text{\rm supp}(w)
the set of integers
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
such that the simple reflection
s_{\alpha_{i}}
appears in some/any reduced expression of
w
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
such that
i\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau})
. Then there are roots
\alpha,\beta\geqslant\alpha_{i}
such that
w_{\tau}^{-1}(\alpha)<0
and
w_{\tau}(\beta)<0
. As a consequence,
a_{\tau,i}-a_{\tau,i+1}<n
and
a_{\tau+1,i}-a_{\tau+1,i+1}<n
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from that
i\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau})
. Let
\alpha=\alpha_{j,j^{\prime}}\in\Phi^{+}
such that
w_{\tau}^{-1}(\alpha)<0
and
\alpha_{i}\leqslant\alpha
. In other words,
j\leqslant i<i+1\leqslant j^{\prime}
and
w_{\tau}^{-1}(j)>w_{\tau}^{-1}(j^{\prime})
. Then we have
a_{\tau+1,i}-a_{\tau+1,i+1}\leqslant a_{\tau+1,j}-a_{\tau+1,j^{\prime}}<n
by Lemma
3.8
. The inequality
a_{\tau,i}-a_{\tau,i+1}<n
follows in a similar way.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

We have
\dim X_{{\mu_{\bullet}}}^{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}\rangle-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})
. Here
\rho_{\bullet}
denotes the half sum of positive roots of
G^{d}
.

Proof.

We set

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\{(\tau,i,j);1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n,{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(j)-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}(i)=1\}

\displaystyle E^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{(\tau,i,j)\in E;a_{\tau,i}-a_{\tau,j}>n\}

\displaystyle E^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\{(\tau,i,j);1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n,w_{\tau}(i)>w_{\tau}(j)\}.

Then
E=E^{\prime}\sqcup E^{\prime\prime}
by Lemma
3.8
. Applying Proposition
2.9
(3) we have

\dim X_{{\mu_{\bullet}}}^{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})=|E^{\prime}|=|E|-|E^{\prime\prime}|=\langle\rho_{\bullet},\overline{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}}-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}\rangle-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}\rangle-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}),

where
\overline{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}}
denotes the dominant conjugate of
{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}
, which equals
{\mu_{\bullet}}
by Proposition
2.9
.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

If
{\lambda_{\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\mu_{\bullet}},{b_{\bullet}}}^{\rm top}
, then
\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})=\sum_{\tau=1}^{d}\ell(w_{\tau})=n-1
, and
w_{d}\cdots w_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
is a product of distinct simple reflections.

Proof.

Let
{\lambda}_{m,n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
{\lambda}_{m,n}(i)=\lfloor\frac{im}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{(i-1)m}{n}\rfloor
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. As
{\lambda_{\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\mu_{\bullet}},{b_{\bullet}}}^{\rm top}
, It follows from
[
18
, §4.4]
that
\sum_{\tau=1}^{d}{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau}={\lambda}_{m,n}
and
\dim X_{{\mu_{\bullet}}}^{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}\rangle-\frac{n-1}{2}
. By Lemma
3.10
,

\displaystyle\dim X_{{\mu_{\bullet}}}^{{\lambda_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})

\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}-{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\bullet}\rangle-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})

\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}\rangle-\langle\rho,{\lambda}_{m,n}\rangle-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})

\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}\rangle+\frac{n-1}{2}-\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})

\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{\bullet},{\mu_{\bullet}}\rangle-\frac{n-1}{2},

where
\rho
is the half sum of positive roots of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. Thus
\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})=n-1
. Moreover, by (
\ast
) we see that
\epsilon_{1}(i)-\epsilon_{1}w_{d}\cdots w_{1}(i)\equiv a_{1,i}-a_{1,w_{d}\cdots w_{1}(i)}\equiv m\mod n
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. So
w_{d}\cdots w_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
acts on
\{1,\dots,n\}
transitively as
m
is coprime to
n
. This means that
n-1\leqslant\ell(w_{d}\cdots w_{1})\leqslant\ell(w_{{\lambda_{\bullet}}})=n-1
and
w_{d}\cdots w_{1}
is a product of distinct simple reflections as desired.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
{\lambda_{\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\mu_{\bullet}},{b_{\bullet}}}^{\rm top}
. Let
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d-1
and
i\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau})
. Then
\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{\iota}\langle\alpha_{i},{\lambda}^{\flat}_{k}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
for
\tau+1\leqslant\iota\leqslant d
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.11
,
i\notin\text{\rm supp}(w_{l})
for
\tau+1\leqslant l\leqslant d
, and moreover, there exists at most one integer
\tau+1\leqslant\tau^{\prime}\leqslant\iota
(resp.
\tau+1\leqslant\tau^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\iota
) such that
i-1\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau^{\prime}})
(resp.
i+1\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau^{\prime\prime}})
). Without loss of generality, we assume such
\tau^{\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}
exist. Therefore,

(i) for
\tau+1\leqslant k\leqslant\iota
we have: (1)
w_{k}(i)\neq i
if and only if
k=\tau^{\prime}
and
w_{\tau^{\prime}}(i)<i
; (2)
w_{k}(i+1)\neq i+1
if and only if
k=\tau^{\prime\prime}
and
w_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(i+1)>i+1
.

Using (i) and the equality from (
\ast
)

\langle\alpha_{i},{\lambda}^{\flat}_{k}\rangle=(a_{k+1,w_{k}(i)}-a_{k+1,w_{k}(i+1)})-(a_{k,i}-a_{k,i+1})

we deduce that

\displaystyle\quad\ \sum_{k=\tau+1}^{\iota}\langle\alpha_{i},{\lambda}^{\flat}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{\tau^{\prime}+1,w_{\tau^{\prime}}(i)}-a_{\tau^{\prime}+1,i}}{n}+\frac{a_{\iota+1,i}-a_{\iota+1,i+1}}{n}+\frac{a_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+1,i+1}-a_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+1,w_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(i+1)}}{n}-\frac{a_{\tau+1,i}-a_{\tau+1,i+1}}{n}

\displaystyle\geqslant 0,

where the inequality follows from that
a_{\tau+1,i}-a_{\tau+1,i+1}<n
(by Lemma
3.9
) and that
a_{\tau^{\prime}+1,w_{\tau^{\prime}}(i)}-a_{\tau^{\prime}+1,i},a_{\iota+1,i}-a_{\iota+1,i+1},a_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+1,i+1}-a_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+1,w_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(i+1)}>0
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.5
.

By Lemma
3.7
we have
{\lambda}^{\natural}_{k}=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}(w_{k-1}\cdots w_{a})^{-1}({\lambda}^{\flat}_{k})
and
U_{{\lambda}_{k}}={}^{\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}(w_{k-1}\cdots w_{a})^{-1}}U
for
a\leqslant k\leqslant d
. By Lemma
3.6
,

\displaystyle\quad\ t^{-{\lambda}_{a}}H_{a}({\lambda_{\bullet}})t^{{\lambda}_{a}^{\dagger}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}t^{-{\lambda}_{c}}H_{c}({\lambda_{\bullet}})t^{{\lambda}_{c}^{\dagger}}K/K

\displaystyle=K_{U_{{\lambda}_{a}}}t^{{\lambda}^{\natural}_{a}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U_{{\lambda}_{c}}}t^{{\lambda}^{\natural}_{c}}K/K

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{a}}\times_{K}w_{a}^{-1}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{a+1}}w_{a}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}(w_{c-1}\cdots w_{a})^{-1}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}w_{c-1}\cdots w_{a}K/K

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{a}}\times_{K}w_{a}^{-1}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{a+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}w_{c-1}^{-1}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K

Therefore, it suffices to show that for
a\leqslant\tau\leqslant c-1
and
i\in\text{\rm supp}(w_{\tau})
we have

s_{i}\overline{K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K}=\overline{K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K}.

Set
U_{i}=U_{\alpha_{i}}
,
U_{-i}=U_{-\alpha_{i}}
and
U^{i}=\prod_{0<\alpha\neq\alpha_{i}}U_{\alpha}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
. Then
U=U_{i}U^{i}=U^{i}U_{i}
and
U^{i}
is normalized by
U_{i}
and
U_{-i}
. As we can take
s_{i}=U_{i}(-1)U_{-i}(1)U_{i}(-1)
, the displayed equality above is equivalent to

U_{-i}(1)\overline{K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K}=\overline{K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K}.

Define
f_{\iota}
for
\tau\leqslant\iota\leqslant d
such that
f_{\tau}=1
and
f_{\iota}=t^{e_{\iota}}f_{\iota-1}/(1+z_{\iota}f_{\iota-1})
with
e_{\iota}=\langle\alpha_{i},{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\iota}\rangle
for
\tau+1\leqslant\iota\leqslant c
. We claim that

(i) for generic points
(z_{\tau+1},\dots,z_{c})\in(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}})^{c-\tau}
we have
1+z_{\iota}f_{\iota-1}\in\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}^{\times}
and
f_{\iota}\in t^{\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{\iota}e_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}
for
\tau+1\leqslant\iota\leqslant c
.

if
\iota=\tau+1
, the claim is true by taking
z_{\tau+1}\in\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}}\setminus\{-1\}
. Suppose it is true for
\iota-1
. Then
f_{\iota-1}\in t^{\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{\iota-1}e_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}
by Lemma
3.12
. So there exist generic points
z_{\iota}\in\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}}
such that

1+z_{\iota}f_{\iota-1}\in\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}^{\times}
and hence
f_{\iota}=t^{e_{\iota}}f_{\iota-1}/(1+z_{\iota}f_{\iota-1})\in t^{\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{\iota}e_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{{\breve{F}}}
as desired. The claim (i) is proved.

Let
(z_{\tau+1},\dots,z_{c})\in(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}})^{c-\tau}
be a generic point as in (i). Using (i) and the commutator relation

U_{-\alpha}(f)U_{\alpha}(z)=U_{\alpha}(\frac{z}{1+zf})(1+zf)^{-\alpha^{\vee}}U_{-\alpha}(\frac{f}{1+zf})\text{ for }1+zf\neq 0,

we deduce that

\displaystyle\quad\ U_{-i}(1)K_{U^{i}}U_{i}(z_{\tau+1})t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U^{i}}U_{i}(z_{c})t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K

\displaystyle\subseteq K_{B}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}U_{-i}(f_{\tau+1})K_{U^{i}}U_{i}(z_{\tau+2})t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+2}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U^{i}}U_{i}(z_{c})t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K

\displaystyle\ \vdots

\displaystyle\subseteq K_{B}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{B}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K

\displaystyle=K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K.

Therefore,
K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K
contains an open dense subset of
U_{-i}(1)K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{\tau+1}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{{\lambda}^{\flat}_{c}}K/K
as desired.
∎

3.5.
The general case

Finally we consider the general case where
{\mu_{\bullet}}\in Y^{d}
is an arbitrary dominant cocharacter. The strategy is to reduce it to the minuscule case considered in the previous subsection.

As
G={\rm Res}_{E/F}{\rm GL}_{n}
, there exist
e\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant d}
, a minuscule dominant cocharacter
{\upsilon_{\bullet}}\in Y^{e}
and a sequence
\Sigma
of integers
1=k_{1}<\cdots<k_{d}<k_{d+1}=e+1
such that
\mu_{\tau}=\upsilon_{k_{\tau}}+\cdots+\upsilon_{k_{\tau+1}-1}
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
. Let
{\rm pr}_{\Sigma}:G^{e}\to G^{d}
be the projection given by
(g_{1},\dots,g_{e})\mapsto(g_{k_{1}},\dots,g_{k_{d}})
. By abuse of notation, we still denote by
{b_{\bullet}}
the element
(1,\dots,1,b)
in
G^{e}({\breve{F}})
and by
\sigma_{\bullet}
the Frobenius of
G^{e}
given by
(g_{1},\dots,g_{e})\mapsto(g_{2},\dots,g_{e},\sigma(g_{1}))
. Then there is a Cartesian square

\textstyle{X_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{G({\breve{F}})\times_{K}{\rm Gr}_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textrm{id}\times_{K}m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\cup_{{\eta_{\bullet}}\leq{\mu_{\bullet}}}X_{{\eta_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G({\breve{F}})\times_{K}{\rm Gr}_{{\mu_{\bullet}}},}

where
m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\Sigma}:{\rm Gr}_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}}}\to{\rm Gr}_{\mu_{\bullet}}
is the partial convolution map given by

(g_{1},\dots,g_{e-1},g_{e}K)\mapsto(g_{k_{1}}\cdots g_{k_{2}-1},\dots,g_{k_{d-1}}\cdots g_{k_{d}-1},g_{k_{d}}\cdots g_{e}K);

the top horizontal map is given by

(g_{1}K,\dots,g_{e}K)\mapsto(g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}g_{2},\dots,g_{e-1}^{-1}g_{e},g_{e}^{-1}b\sigma(g_{1})K);

the bottom horizontal map is given by

(h_{1}K,\dots,h_{d}K)\mapsto(h_{1},h_{1}^{-1}h_{2},\dots,h_{d-1}^{-1}h_{d},h_{d}^{-1}b\sigma(h_{1})K).

For a dominant cocharacter
{\eta_{\bullet}}\in Y^{d}
we denote by
m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\eta_{\bullet}}
the multiplicity with which
V_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}
appears in
V_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{e}}
. Here we view each
\widehat{G}^{e}
-crystal as a
\widehat{G}^{d}
-crystal via the embedding
\widehat{G}^{d}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}^{e}
given by
(h_{1},\dots,h_{d})\mapsto(h_{1}^{(k_{2}-k_{1})},\dots,h_{d}^{(k_{d+1}-k_{d})})
.

Proposition 3.13
.

We have

|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})|=\sum_{{\eta_{\bullet}}\leq{\mu_{\bullet}}}m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\eta_{\bullet}}|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{{\eta_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})|.

As a consequence,
|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})|=\dim V_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
.

Proof.

The first statement follows similarly as Corollary
1.6
. To show the second one, we argue by induction on
|{\mu_{\bullet}}|
. If
{\mu_{\bullet}}
minuscule, it is proved in Theorem
3.3
. Suppose it is true for
|{\eta_{\bullet}}|<|{\mu_{\bullet}}|
. By the choice of
{\upsilon_{\bullet}}
we have
m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\mu_{\bullet}}=1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})|

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\eta_{\bullet}}\leq{\mu_{\bullet}}}m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\eta_{\bullet}}|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{{\eta_{\bullet}}}({b_{\bullet}})|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})|+\sum_{{\eta_{\bullet}}<{\mu_{\bullet}}}m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\eta_{\bullet}}\dim V_{\eta_{\bullet}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\eta_{\bullet}}\leq{\mu_{\bullet}}}m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\eta_{\bullet}}\dim V_{\eta_{\bullet}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)),

where the second equality follows from the induction hypothesis, and the last equality follows again from Theorem
3.3
as
{\upsilon_{\bullet}}
is minuscule. Therefore, we have
|\mathbb{J}_{{b_{\bullet}}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})|=\dim V_{\mu_{\bullet}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
as desired.
∎

Similar to the definition of
\otimes
in Theorem
0.7
, let
\otimes_{\Sigma}:\mathbb{B}_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{e}}\to\sqcup_{{\eta_{\bullet}}}\mathbb{B}_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}
denote the map given by

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{e})\mapsto(\delta_{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\delta_{k_{2}-1},\dots,\delta_{k_{d}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\delta_{k_{d+1}-1}).

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Let
C\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})
. By Theorem
3.3
and Proposition
3.13
, there exists
{\xi_{\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\upsilon_{\bullet}},{b_{\bullet}}}^{\rm top}
such that
\xi^{\flat}_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{B}_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{e}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
and
C={\rm pr}_{\Sigma}(\overline{X_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\xi_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})})
. Write
{\xi_{\bullet}}=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{e})
,
\xi_{\bullet}^{\dagger}={b_{\bullet}}\sigma_{\bullet}({\xi_{\bullet}})=(\xi_{1}^{\dagger},\dots,\xi_{e}^{\dagger})
and
\xi_{\bullet}^{\flat}=(\xi_{1}^{\flat},\dots,\xi_{e}^{\flat})
. Define

\gamma^{G^{d}}(C)=\otimes_{\Sigma}(\xi_{\bullet}^{\flat})=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})\in\sqcup_{\eta_{\bullet}}\mathbb{B}_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)),

where
\gamma_{\tau}=\xi_{k_{\tau}}^{\flat}\otimes\cdots\otimes\xi_{k_{\tau+1}-1}^{\flat}\in\mathbb{B}^{\widehat{G}}:=\sqcup_{\eta}\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{G}}
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
.

Let
{\lambda_{\bullet}}={\rm pr}_{\Sigma}({\xi_{\bullet}})\in Y^{d}
. Then
(I^{d}t^{\lambda_{\bullet}}K^{d}/K^{d})\cap C
is open dense in
C
. So

\overline{(\theta_{\lambda_{\bullet}}^{G^{d}})^{-1}(C)}=\overline{{\rm pr}_{\Sigma}((\theta_{\xi_{\bullet}}^{G^{e}})^{-1}(X_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\xi_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})))}\subseteq I^{d},

which means (by the proof of Lemma
2.10
) that, for each
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
,

\overline{H_{\tau}(C)}=\overline{H_{k_{\tau}}({\xi_{\bullet}})\cdots H_{k_{\tau+1}-1}({\xi_{\bullet}})},

where
H_{\tau}(C)
and
H({\xi_{\bullet}})
are defined in §
2.3
and §
3.3
respectively. Thus for
1\leqslant a\leqslant c\leqslant d
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \overline{t^{-{\lambda}_{a}}H_{a}(C)t^{{\lambda}_{a}^{\dagger}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}t^{-{\lambda}_{c}}H_{c}(C)t^{{\lambda}_{c}^{\dagger}}K/K}

\displaystyle=\overline{m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\Sigma}(t^{-\xi_{k_{a}}}H_{k_{a}}({\xi_{\bullet}})t^{\xi_{k_{a}}^{\dagger}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}t^{-\xi_{k_{c+1}-1}}H_{k_{c+1}-1}({\xi_{\bullet}})t^{\xi_{k_{c+1}-1}^{\dagger}}K/K)}

\displaystyle=\overline{m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\Sigma}(\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}K_{U}t^{\xi_{k_{a}}^{\flat}}\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}K_{U}t^{\xi_{k_{c+1}-1}^{\flat}}K/K)}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{a}}\overline{S_{\gamma_{a}}\tilde{\times}\cdots\tilde{\times}S_{\gamma_{c}}},

where the first equality follows from that
{\lambda}_{\tau}=\xi_{k_{\tau}}
and
{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\tau}=\xi^{\dagger}_{k_{\tau+1}-1}
for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
; the second equality follows from Proposition
3.5
. In particular, we have

\overline{t^{-{\lambda}_{\tau}}H_{\tau}(C)t^{{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\dagger}}K/K}=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{\tau}}\overline{S_{\gamma_{\tau}}}

by taking
a=c=\tau
. On the other hand, as
C\subseteq X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})
it follows that

t^{-{\lambda}_{\tau}}H_{\tau}(C)t^{{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\dagger}}K/K\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{\mu_{\tau}}^{\circ}.

Thus,
\gamma_{\tau}\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\tau}}^{\widehat{G}}
and hence
\gamma^{G^{d}}(C)\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
. Now the first statement of Theorem
3.1
follows by taking
a=1
and
c=d
.

As
{b_{\bullet}}
is superbasic,
\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}}=(\Omega^{d}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}})(I^{d}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}})
. By Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
2.5
, the map
C\mapsto\gamma^{G^{d}}(C)
defined in the previous paragraph induces a map

\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})\to\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)).

Then we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm Irr}X_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{\Sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{G^{e}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{e}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\otimes_{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\sqcup_{\eta_{\bullet}}{\rm Irr}X_{\eta_{\bullet}}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{G^{d}}}
\textstyle{\sqcup_{\eta_{\bullet}}\mathbb{B}_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)).}

As
\gamma^{G^{e}}
is bijective and
m_{\upsilon_{\bullet}}^{\mu_{\bullet}}=1
, the map
\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})\overset{\gamma^{G^{d}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
is surjective and hence bijective by Proposition
3.13
.
∎

4.
Proof of Theorem
0.5
and
0.7

4.1.
Irreducible components of
S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}

Let
P=MN
and
b\in M({\breve{F}})
be as in §
2.1
. For
\mu\in Y^{+}
let
I_{\mu,M}
be the set of
M
-dominant cocharacters
\eta
such that

S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}:=N({\breve{F}})t^{\eta}K/K\cap{\rm Gr}_{\mu}^{\circ}\neq\emptyset.

Define
I_{\mu,b,M}=\{\eta\in I_{\mu,M};\eta=\kappa_{M}(b)\in\pi_{1}(M)_{\sigma}\}
.

Proposition 4.1

(
[
9
, Proposition 5.4.2]
)
.

Let
\eta\in I_{\mu,M}
, then

\dim S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}\leqslant\langle\rho,\mu+\eta\rangle-2\langle\rho_{M},\eta\rangle.

Moreover, let
\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
be the set of irreducible components of
S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
with the maximal possible dimension
\langle\rho,\mu+\eta\rangle-2\langle\rho_{M},\eta\rangle
. Then
|\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}|
equals the multiplicity with which
\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}
appears in
\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}
.

For
\eta\in I_{\mu,M}
recall that
\theta_{\eta}^{N}:N({\breve{F}})\to{\rm Gr}_{P}
is the map given by
n\mapsto nt^{\eta}K_{P}
. Let
Z^{N}\in{\rm Irr}S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
and
g=ht^{\eta}h^{\prime}\in K_{M}t^{\eta}K_{M}
with
h,h^{\prime}\in K_{M}
. We define

\displaystyle(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g

\displaystyle=h(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})h^{-1}g\subseteq P({\breve{F}});

\displaystyle Z^{N}\ast(gK_{M})

\displaystyle=(\theta_{\eta}^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g)K_{P}/K_{P}\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{P},

which do not depend on the choices of
h,h^{\prime}\in K_{M}
since the connected group
K_{M}\cap t^{\eta}K_{M}t^{-\eta}
fixes
S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
and hence fixes each of its irreducible components, by left multiplication. For
\mathcal{D}^{M}\subseteq K_{M}t^{\eta}K_{M}
we set

\displaystyle(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast\mathcal{D}^{M}

\displaystyle=\cup_{g\in\mathcal{D}^{M}}(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g;

\displaystyle Z^{N}\ast(\mathcal{D}^{M}K_{M}/K_{M})

\displaystyle=\cup_{g\in\mathcal{D}^{M}}Z^{N}\ast(gK_{M}).

Notice that
Z^{N}\ast(\mathcal{D}^{M}K_{M}/K_{M})=((\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast\mathcal{D}^{M})K_{P}/K_{P}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
Z^{N}\in{\rm Irr}S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
and
g\in K_{M}t^{\eta}K_{M}
. Then we have

(1)
h((\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g)=(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast(hg)
for
h\in K_{M}
;

(2)
u((\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g)=(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast g
for
u\in K_{N}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows by definition. The second one follows from that
K_{N}Z^{N}=Z^{N}
since
K_{N}
fixes
S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
and hence fixes each of its irreducible components by left multiplication.
∎

4.2.
Iwasawa decomposition of
X_{\mu}(b)

Notice that the natural projection
P=MN\to M
induces a map

\beta:X_{\mu}(b)\hookrightarrow{\rm Gr}_{G}={\rm Gr}_{P}\to{\rm Gr}_{M}.

Let
\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}
and let
X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
be the affine Deligne-Lusztig variety defined for
M
. For
Z^{N}\in{\rm Irr}S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
and
C^{M}\subseteq X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
we define

X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)=\{gK_{P}\in\beta^{-1}(C^{M});g^{-1}b\sigma(g)K_{P}\in Z^{N}\ast{\rm Gr}_{\eta,M}^{\circ}\}\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{P},

where
{\rm Gr}_{\eta,M}^{\circ}=K_{M}t^{\eta}K_{M}/K_{M}
. Notice that the natural projection

Z^{N}\ast{\rm Gr}_{\eta,M}^{\circ}\to{\rm Gr}_{\eta,M}^{\circ}

is a fiber bundle with fibers isomorphic to
Z^{N}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
C^{M}\subseteq X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
be locally closed and irreducible. Then

(1)
\beta^{-1}(C^{M})=\cup_{Z^{N}\in{\rm Irr}S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}}X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
;

(2)
\dim X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)\leqslant\dim X_{\mu}(b)
, where the equality holds if and only if
\dim C^{M}=\dim X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
and
Z^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
;

(3)
N({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
acts transitively on
{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
;

(4)
X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
is irreducible if
M
is the centralizer of
\nu_{b}
;

(5)
t^{-{\lambda}}H^{P}(C)t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}=(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast(t^{-{\lambda}}H^{M}(C^{M})t^{b\sigma({\lambda})})
if
C\in{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
and
C^{M}\in{\rm Irr}X^{{\lambda},M}_{\eta}
for some
{\lambda}\in Y
.

Here
X_{\eta}^{{\lambda},M}(b)=I_{M}t^{\lambda}K_{M}/K_{M}\cap X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
, and
H^{M}(C^{M})
is define in §
2.1
for
G=M
.

Proof.

Let
mK_{M}\in X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
. By definition,

\beta^{-1}(mK_{M})=\{mnK_{P};n^{-1}m^{-1}b\sigma(m)\sigma(n)K_{P}\in S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}\ast(b_{m}K_{M})\}.

So the statement (1) follows. Moreover, as
\nu_{M}(b)=\nu_{G}(b)
is dominant, it follows from
[
9
, Proposition 5.3.2]
that

\dim(X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)\cap\beta^{-1}(mK_{M}))=\dim Z^{N}-\langle 2\rho_{N},\eta\rangle.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.1
we have

\displaystyle\dim X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)

\displaystyle=\dim C^{M}+\dim Z^{N}-2\langle\rho,\eta\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\langle\rho_{M},\eta\rangle-\frac{1}{2}{\rm def}_{M}(b)+\langle\rho,\mu+\eta\rangle-2\langle\rho_{M},\eta\rangle-2\langle\rho_{N},\eta\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\rho,\mu\rangle-\langle\rho_{N},\eta\rangle-\frac{1}{2}{\rm def}_{G}(b)

\displaystyle=\langle\rho,\mu-\nu_{M}(b)\rangle-\frac{1}{2}{\rm def}_{G}(b)

\displaystyle=\dim X_{\mu}(b),

where the equality holds if and only if
\dim C^{M}=\dim X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
and
Z^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
. So the statement (2) follows.

The statement (3) follows similarly as
[
18
, Proposition 5.6]
which deals with the minuscule case. Notice that for minuscule
\mu
the sets
S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}=K_{N}t^{\eta}K/K
and
N({\breve{F}})\cap t^{-\eta}Kt^{\mu}K=t^{-\eta}(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(S_{\mu,\eta}^{N})t^{\eta}
are irreducible. For general case we only needs to replace
N({\breve{F}})\cap t^{-\eta}Kt^{\mu}K
in
[
18
, Claim 1 on page 1630]
with the irreducible set
t^{-\eta}(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})t^{\eta}
for
Z^{N}\in{\rm Irr}S_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
.

The statement (4) follows from the statement (3) and that
N({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}=\{1\}
.

It follows by definition that

t^{-{\lambda}}H^{P}(X^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b))t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}=(\theta_{\eta}^{N})^{-1}(Z^{N})\ast(t^{-{\lambda}}H^{M}(C^{M})t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}).

By the statement (3), all the irreducible components of
X^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
are conjugate under
N({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
. Thus
H^{P}(C)=H^{P}(X^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b))
for
C\in{\rm Irr}X^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)
, and the statement (5) follows.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

The map

(Z^{N},C^{M})\mapsto\mathbb{J}_{b}^{M}{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)}=(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}){\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{M}}(b)}

induces a bijection

(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)\cong\sqcup_{\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}}\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}\times(\mathbb{J}_{b}^{M}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\eta}^{M}(b)).

As a consequence,
|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|\leqslant\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
.

Proof.

The bijection follows from Proposition
4.3
(1), (2), (3). Choose
P=MN
such that
b
is superbasic in
M({\breve{F}})
. Then

\displaystyle|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|

\displaystyle\leqslant|(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}}|\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}||\mathbb{J}_{b}^{M}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\eta}^{M}(b)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}}|\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}|\dim V_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{M}(b))

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{M}(b))

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)),

where the second equality follows from Proposition
3.13
dealing with the subperbasic case, and the last one follows from that
{\underline{\lambda}}_{M}(b)={\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)
.
∎

4.3.
The numerical identity

In this subsection we prove the numerical version of Theorem
0.5
.

Proposition 4.5
.

We have
|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
if
\mu
is minuscule and
b
is basic.

The proof is given in §
5
.

Lemma 4.6
.

If
G
is simple, adjoint and has some nonzero minuscule cocharacter, then each irreducible
\widehat{G}
-module appears in some tensor product of irreducible
\widehat{G}
-modules with minuscule highest weights.

Proof.

Let
\mu\in Y^{+}
. By the assumption on
G
, there exists a dominant and minuscule cocharacter
{\mu_{\bullet}}\in Y^{d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
such that
\mu\leq|{\mu_{\bullet}}|
and hence
{\rm Gr}_{\mu}\subseteq m_{\mu_{\bullet}}({\rm Gr}_{\mu_{\bullet}})
. By Theorem
1.5
(2),
V_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}
appears in
V_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}}
as desired.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

The condition in Lemma
4.6
is equivalent to that
G
is simple, adjoint, and any/some of its absolute factors is of classical type or
E_{6}
type or
E_{7}
type.

Proposition 4.8
.

We have
|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
if
b
is basic.

First we reduce Proposition
4.8
to the adjoint case.

Lemma 4.9
.

Proposition
4.8
is true for
G
if it is true for
G=G_{\rm ad}
.

Proof.

Choose
\omega\in\pi_{1}(G)
such that
X_{\mu}(b)^{\omega}:=X_{\mu}(b)\cap{\rm Gr}^{\omega}\neq\emptyset
, where
{\rm Gr}^{\omega}
is the corresponding connected component of
{\rm Gr}
. By
[
3
, Corollary 2.4.2]
and
[
18
, Proposition 3.1]
, the natural projection
G\to G_{\rm ad}
induces a universal homeomorphism
X_{\mu}(b)^{\omega}\overset{\sim}{\to}X_{\mu_{\rm ad}}(b_{\rm ad})^{\omega_{\rm ad}}
, where
\mu_{\rm ad}
,
b_{\rm ad}
and
\omega_{\rm ad}
denote the images of
\mu
,
b
and
\omega
respectively under the natural projection
G\to G_{\rm ad}
. Let
\mathbb{J}_{b}^{0}
,
\mathbb{J}_{b_{\rm ad}}^{0}
be the kernels of the natural projections
\mathbb{J}_{b}\to\pi_{1}(G)
,
\mathbb{J}_{b_{\rm ad}}\to\pi_{1}(G_{\rm ad})
respectively. Notice that
X_{\mu}(b)=(\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})X_{\mu}(b)^{\omega}
as
b\in\Omega
is basic. By Corollary
4.4
,

\displaystyle\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))

\displaystyle\geqslant|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|=|\mathbb{J}_{b}^{0}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)^{\omega}|

\displaystyle\geqslant|\mathbb{J}_{b_{\rm ad}}^{0}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\rm ad}}(b_{\rm ad})^{\omega_{\rm ad}}|=|\mathbb{J}_{b_{\rm ad}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\rm ad}}(b_{\rm ad})|

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\mu_{\rm ad}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G_{\rm ad}}(b_{\rm ad}))=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)),

where the second last equality follows by assumption.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.8
by assuming Proposition
4.5
.

By Lemma
4.9
, we can assume
G
is adjoint and simple. If the coweight lattice equals the coroot lattice, then
b
is unramified and the statement is proved in
[
56
, Theorem 4.4.14]
. So we will assume
G
has a nonzero minuscule coweight. By Lemma
4.6
, there exists a minuscule and dominant cocharacter
{\mu_{\bullet}}\in Y^{d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
such that
\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}
appears in
\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}}
, that is,
m_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\mu}\neq 0
. By Proposition
4.5
,

\displaystyle\dim V_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{J}_{b_{\bullet}}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\upsilon\leq|{\mu_{\bullet}}|}m_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\upsilon}|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}X_{\upsilon}(b)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\upsilon\leq|{\mu_{\bullet}}|}m_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\upsilon}\dim V_{\upsilon}^{\widehat{G}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)).

where the second equality follows from Corollary
1.6
, and the inequality follows from Corollary
4.4
. Thus
|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}X_{\upsilon}(b)|=\dim V_{\upsilon}({\underline{\lambda}}(b))
if
m_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\upsilon}\neq 0
.
∎

Theorem 4.10
.

We have
|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
. In particular,

(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)\cong\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b).

Proof.

By Corollary
4.4
we have

\displaystyle|(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}}|\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}||\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\eta}^{M}({\lambda})|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}}|\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}|\dim V_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{M}(b))

\displaystyle=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)),

where the second equality follows from Proposition
4.8
since
b
is basic in
M({\breve{F}})
. Now the first statement follows by taking
M
to be the centralizer of
\nu_{G}(b)
, in which case
P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}=\mathbb{J}_{b}^{M}=\mathbb{J}_{b}
. The second statement follows from the equality
|(P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|=\dim V_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))=|\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)|
.
∎

4.4.
Decomposition of MV-cycles

Notice that each
{\widehat{G}}
-crystal restricts to an
\widehat{M}
-crystal. For
\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}
we denote by
S_{\delta}^{M}
the corresponding Mirković-Vilonen cycle in
{\rm Gr}_{M}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}
and let
\eta\in I_{\mu,M}
such that
\delta
lies in a highest weight
\widehat{M}
-crystal isomorphic to
\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}
. Then there exists a unique irreducible component
Z_{\delta}^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
such that

\overline{S_{\delta}}=\overline{Z_{\delta}^{N}\ast S_{\delta}^{M}}.

Here we view
S_{\delta}^{M}
as its open dense subset lying in
{\rm Gr}_{\eta,M}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

Let
\chi\in I_{\mu,M}
. Let
Z^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\chi}^{N}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{\chi}^{\widehat{M}}({\lambda})
for some
{\lambda}\in Y
. Then
Z^{N}\ast S_{\xi}^{M}\subseteq S^{\lambda}
is irreducible as the natural projection
Z^{N}\ast S_{\xi}^{M}\to S_{\xi}^{M}
is a fiber bundle with fibers isomorphic to
Z^{N}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\dim Z^{N}\ast S_{\xi}^{M}

\displaystyle=\dim Z^{N}+\dim S_{\xi}^{M}

\displaystyle=\langle\rho,\mu+\chi\rangle-2\langle\rho_{M},\chi\rangle+\langle\rho_{M},\chi+{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\rho,\mu+{\lambda}\rangle+\langle\rho_{N},\chi-{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\rho,\mu+{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\dim(S^{\lambda}\cap{\rm Gr}_{\mu}),

where the last equality follows from that
\chi-{\lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}\Phi_{M}^{\vee}
. Therefore,

\overline{Z\ast S_{\xi}^{M}}\in{\rm Irr}(\overline{S^{\lambda}\cap{\rm Gr}_{\mu}})\cong{\rm MV}_{\mu}({\lambda})=\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}({\lambda}).

Hence the map
(Z^{N},\xi)\mapsto\overline{Z^{N}\ast S_{\xi}^{M}}
gives an embedding

\sqcup_{\chi}(\Sigma_{\mu,\chi}^{N}\times\mathbb{B}_{\chi}^{\widehat{M}})\hookrightarrow{\rm MV}_{\mu}\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}},

which is bijective since
\sum_{\chi}|\Sigma_{\mu,\chi}^{N}||\mathbb{B}_{\chi}^{\widehat{M}}|=|\mathbb{B}_{\mu}^{\widehat{G}}|
by Proposition
4.1
. Thus there exist unique
\kappa\in I_{\mu,M}
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{B}_{\kappa}^{\widehat{M}}
and
Z_{\delta}^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\kappa}^{N}
such that
\overline{S_{\delta}}=\overline{Z_{\delta}^{N}\ast S_{\zeta}^{M}}
. It remains to show
\zeta=\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}
, that is,
\overline{\pi_{P}(S_{\delta})}=\overline{S_{\delta}^{M}}
with
\pi_{P}:{\rm Gr}_{P}\to{\rm Gr}_{M}
the natural projection. In view of the construction of MV cycles using Littelmann’s path model
[
56
, Proposition 3.3.12 & 3.3.15]
, it suffices to consider the case where
\mu
is a quasi-minuscule cocharacter of
G
. Then the statement follows from the explicit construction in
[
56
, §3.2.5 & Definition 3.3.6]
.
∎

4.5.
Proof of Theorem
0.5

Take
P=MN
such that
b
is superbasic in
M({\breve{F}})
. Let
C\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)
. By Corollary
4.4
, there exist
\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}
and
{\lambda}\in Y
such that
C\subseteq\overline{X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{{\lambda},M}}(b)}
for some
(Z^{N},C^{{\lambda},M})\in\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}\times{\rm Irr}X_{\eta}^{{\lambda},M}
such that
\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}}\in{\rm Irr}X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
. In particular,
(N({\breve{F}})I_{M}t^{\lambda}K/K)\cap C
is open dense in
C
. Let
\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})\in\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{M}(b))
be as in Theorem
3.1
such that

\overline{t^{-{\lambda}}H^{M}(C^{{\lambda},M})t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}K_{M}/K_{M}}=\epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}\overline{S^{M}_{\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})}}\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{M}.

By Lemma
4.11
, there exists
\gamma(C)\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}(b))
such that

\overline{Z^{N}\ast S_{\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})}^{M}}=\overline{S_{\gamma(C)}}.

By Proposition
4.3
(5),

\overline{t^{-{\lambda}}H^{P}(C)t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}K/K}=\overline{Z^{N}\ast\epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}S_{\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})}}=\epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}\overline{Z^{N}\ast S_{\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})}}=\epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}\overline{S_{\gamma(C)}}.

So the first statement follows.

Let
C^{\prime}\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)
be a conjugate of
C
under
\mathbb{J}_{b}
. By Theorem
4.10
,
C^{\prime}
and
C
are conjugate under
P({\breve{F}})\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
, which, combined with Corollary
4.4
, implies that
C^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{X_{\mu}^{Z^{N},C^{{\lambda}^{\prime},M}}(b)}
for some
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in Y
and
C^{{\lambda}^{\prime},M}\in{\rm Irr}X_{\eta}^{{\lambda}^{\prime},M}(b)
such that
\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}}
and
\overline{C^{{\lambda}^{\prime},M}}
are conjugate by
\mathbb{J}_{b}^{M}
. By Theorem
3.1
, we have

\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda}^{\prime},M}})=\gamma^{M}(\overline{C^{{\lambda},M}})

and hence
\gamma(C^{\prime})=\gamma(C)
. So
\gamma
is invariant on the
\mathbb{J}_{b}
-orbits of
{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)
.

It remains to show
\gamma
induces a bijection
\mathbb{J}_{b}\backslash{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
. By Theorem
4.10
it suffices to show it is surjective. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
. Suppose
\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b))
for some
\eta\in I_{\mu,b,M}
. It follows from Theorem
3.1
that there exists
C^{M}\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\eta}^{M}(b)
such that

\gamma^{M}(C^{M})=\delta\in\mathbb{B}_{\eta}^{\widehat{M}}({\underline{\lambda}}_{G}(b)).

Let
\phi\in Y
and
C^{\phi,M}\in{\rm Irr}X_{\eta}^{\phi,M}(b)
such that
\overline{C^{\phi,M}}=C^{M}
. Let
Z_{\delta}^{N}\in\Sigma_{\mu,\eta}^{N}
be as in Lemma
4.11
such that
\overline{S_{\delta}}=\overline{Z_{\delta}^{N}\ast S_{\delta}^{M}}
. By the construction in the previous paragraph, we have
\gamma(C)=\delta
for any
C\in{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{Z_{\delta}^{N},C^{\phi,M}}(b)}
. So
\gamma
is surjective as desired.

4.6.
Proof of Theorem
0.7

Let
C\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu_{\bullet}}({b_{\bullet}})
and
C^{\prime}\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\mu}(b)
for some
\mu\in Y^{+}
such that
\overline{C^{\prime}}=\overline{{\rm pr}(C)}
. One should not confuse with the notation in the previous subsection. Assume
\gamma^{G^{d}}(C)=\gamma_{\bullet}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})\in\mathbb{B}_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{\widehat{G}^{d}}
. By Corollary
1.6
, it suffices to show that

\gamma(C^{\prime})=\gamma_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\gamma_{d}.

It follows from Corollary
4.4
that there exist
Z_{\bullet}^{N^{d}}=(Z_{1}^{N},\cdots,Z_{d}^{N})\in\Sigma_{{\mu_{\bullet}},{\eta_{\bullet}}}^{N^{d}}
and
C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}\in{\rm Irr}X_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}({b_{\bullet}})
for some
{\eta_{\bullet}}\in I_{{\mu_{\bullet}},M^{d}}
and
{\lambda_{\bullet}}\in Y^{d}
such that
\overline{C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}}\in{\rm Irr}^{\rm top}X_{\eta_{\bullet}}^{M^{d}}({b_{\bullet}})
and

(i)

\displaystyle C\subseteq\overline{X_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{Z_{\bullet}^{N^{d}},C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}}({b_{\bullet}})}.

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{N^{d}}=(\theta_{{\eta_{\bullet}}}^{N^{d}})^{-1}(Z_{\bullet}^{N^{d}})=(\mathcal{Z}^{N}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Z}_{d}^{N})
. By (i) and Proposition
4.3
(5),

(ii)

\displaystyle\overline{t^{-{\lambda_{\bullet}}}H^{P^{d}}(C)t^{{b_{\bullet}}\sigma_{\bullet}({\lambda_{\bullet}})}}=\overline{\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{N^{d}}\ast(t^{-{\lambda_{\bullet}}}H^{M^{d}}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{b_{\bullet}}\sigma_{\bullet}({\lambda_{\bullet}})})}.

Set
{\lambda_{\bullet}}=({\lambda}_{1},\dots,{\lambda}_{d})
,
{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\bullet}={b_{\bullet}}\sigma_{\bullet}({\lambda_{\bullet}})=({\lambda}_{1}^{\dagger},\dots,{\lambda}_{d}^{\dagger})
and

\displaystyle H^{P^{d}}(C)

\displaystyle=H_{1}(C)\times\cdots\times H_{d}(C);

\displaystyle H^{M^{d}}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})

\displaystyle=H_{1}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})\times\cdots\times H_{d}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}).

Applying Theorem
0.5
(for
C
and
\overline{C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}}}
respectively) and Lemma
4.11
we have

\epsilon_{\lambda_{\bullet}}^{M^{d}}\overline{S_{\gamma_{\bullet}}^{G^{d}}}=\overline{t^{-{\lambda_{\bullet}}}H^{P^{d}}(C)t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\bullet}}K^{d}/K^{d}}=\overline{\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{N^{d}}\ast(t^{-{\lambda_{\bullet}}}H^{M^{d}}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\bullet}})K^{d}/K^{d}}=\epsilon_{\lambda_{\bullet}}^{M^{d}}\overline{Z_{\bullet}^{N^{d}}\ast S_{\gamma_{\bullet}}^{M^{d}}}.

In particular, for
1\leqslant\tau\leqslant d
we have

(iii)

\displaystyle\overline{S_{\gamma_{\tau}}}=(\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{\tau}}^{M})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{Z}^{N}_{\tau}\ast(t^{-{\lambda}_{\tau}}H_{\tau}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\dagger}})K/K}=\overline{Z^{N}_{\tau}\ast S_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{M}}.

Let
{\lambda}={\rm pr}({\lambda_{\bullet}})={\lambda}_{1}
. As
\overline{C^{\prime}}=\overline{{\rm pr}(C)}\subseteq{\rm Gr}
, we see that
N({\breve{F}})I_{M}t^{\lambda}K/K\cap C^{\prime}
is open dense in
C^{\prime}
. By Theorem
0.5
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}\overline{S_{\gamma(C^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle=\overline{t^{-{\lambda}}H^{P}(C^{\prime})t^{b\sigma({\lambda})}K/K}

\displaystyle=\overline{t^{-{\lambda}_{1}}H_{1}(C)t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{1}}\cdots t^{-{\lambda}_{d}}H_{d}(C)t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{d}}K/K}

\displaystyle=\overline{(\mathcal{Z}_{1}^{N}\ast(t^{-{\lambda}_{1}}H_{1}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{1}}))\cdots(\mathcal{Z}_{d}^{N}\ast(t^{-{\lambda}_{d}}H_{d}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{d}}))K/K}

\displaystyle=\overline{m(\mathcal{Z}_{1}^{N}\ast(t^{-{\lambda}_{1}}H_{1}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{1}})\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}(\mathcal{Z}_{d}^{N}\ast(t^{-{\lambda}_{d}}H_{d}(C^{{\lambda_{\bullet}},M^{d}})t^{{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{d}}))K/K)}

\displaystyle=\overline{m(\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{1}}^{M}(Z_{1}^{N}\ast S_{\gamma_{1}}^{M})\tilde{\times}\cdots\tilde{\times}(Z_{d}^{N}\ast S_{\gamma_{d}}^{M}))}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{1}}^{M}\overline{m(S_{\gamma_{1}}\tilde{\times}\cdots\tilde{\times}S_{\gamma_{d}})}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{{\lambda}_{1}}^{M}\overline{S_{\gamma_{1}}\star\cdots\star S_{\gamma_{d}}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\lambda}^{M}\overline{S_{\gamma_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\gamma_{d}}},

where
m:G({\breve{F}})\times_{K}\cdots\times_{K}G({\breve{F}})\times_{K}{\rm Gr}\to{\rm Gr}
is the usual convolution map; the second equality follows from Lemma
2.10
; the third one follows from (ii) and that
{\lambda}^{\dagger}_{\bullet}=({\lambda}_{1}^{\dagger},\dots,{\lambda}_{d}^{\dagger})=({\lambda}_{2},\dots,{\lambda}_{d},b\sigma({\lambda}_{1}))
; the fifth one follows from Lemma
4.2
, Theorem
3.1
and (iii). So
\gamma(C^{\prime})=\gamma_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\gamma_{d}
as desired.

5.
Proof of Proposition
4.5

We keep the notations in §
1
. Let
\mu\in Y^{+}
be minuscule, and let
b\in G({\breve{F}})
be basic which is a lift of an element in
\Omega
. To prove Proposition
4.5
, we assume by Lemma
4.9
that
G
is simple and adjoint. Then
\sigma
acts transitively on the connected components of (the Dynkin diagram of)
\mathbb{S}_{0}
. Let
d
be the number of connected components of
\mathbb{S}_{0}
.

By abuse of notation, we also denote by
{\tilde{w}}\in\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}=\{x\in\tilde{W};b\sigma(x)b^{-1}=x\}
some lift of
{\tilde{w}}
in
N_{T}({\breve{F}})
that lies in
\mathbb{J}_{b}
.

5.1.
Orthogonal subset of roots

We say a subset
D\subseteq\Phi
is strongly orthogonal if
\beta^{\prime}\pm\beta\notin\Phi
for any
\beta^{\prime},\beta\in D
. In particular, if
D
is strongly orthogonal, then it is orthogonal, that is,
\langle\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\vee}\rangle=0
for any
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in D
.

Let
\alpha\in\Phi
. Set
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}=\{\alpha^{i};i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}=\{{\tilde{\alpha}}^{i};i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, where
\alpha^{i}
and
{\tilde{\alpha}}^{i}
are as in §
2.2
. Let
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
be the parabolic subgroup of
\tilde{W}
generated by
s_{\tilde{\beta}}
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. Recall that
\Pi
is the set of minus simple roots and highest roots of
\Phi
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\alpha\in\Pi
such that
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
is finite. Let
{\tilde{w}}
be the longest element of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
. Then
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}=\{1,{\tilde{w}}\}
and one of the following cases occurs:

(1)
\langle\alpha^{d},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=-1
,
|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}|=2d
,
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha+\alpha^{d}}
is strongly orthogonal (as
|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha+\alpha^{d}}|=d
) and
{\tilde{w}}=\prod_{\xi\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha+\alpha^{d}}}s_{\tilde{\xi}}
;

(2)
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
is strongly orthogonal and hence
{\tilde{w}}=\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}s_{\tilde{\beta}}
.

In particular, any affine reflection of
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
is equal to
\prod_{c\in\mathcal{O}_{a}}s_{c}
for some
a=(\gamma,k)\in\Phi\times\mathbb{Z}=\tilde{\Phi}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\gamma}
is strongly orthogonal.

Proof.

The first statement follows from a case-by-case analysis. The “In particular” part follows by noticing that each reflection of
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
is conjugate to some
{\tilde{w}}
as in the first statement.
∎

5.2.
Characterization of
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}

Let
{\lambda}\in Y
. Let
X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)=It^{\lambda}K/K\cap X_{\mu}(b)
,
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}=\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{G}
,
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}=\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{{\rm top},G}
and
R({\lambda})=R_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}({\lambda})
be as in §
2.2
. By Proposition
2.9
,
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
if and only if
{\lambda}^{\natural}=-{\lambda}+b\sigma({\lambda})
is conjugate to
\mu
by
W_{0}
.

Let
V=Y\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
and
V^{p(b\sigma)}=\{v\in V;p(b\sigma)(v)=v\}
. Define

V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}=\{v\in V^{p(b\sigma)};\langle\alpha,v\rangle=0\Leftrightarrow\langle\alpha,V^{p(b\sigma)}\rangle=0,\forall\alpha\in\Phi\},

which is open dense in
V^{p(b\sigma)}
. Notice that
V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}\cap Y\neq\emptyset
. Let
M_{b}\supseteq T
be the Levi subgroup with root system
\{\alpha\in\Phi;\langle\alpha,V^{p(b\sigma)}\rangle=0\}
. By definition, for any
v\in V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}
the centralizer
M_{v}
(see §
1.3
) of
v
in
G
coincides with
M_{b}
.

Fix
v\in V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}\cap Y
. Denote by
\bar{v}
the unique dominant
W_{0}
-conjugate of
v
. Let
z
be the minimal element of
W_{0}
such that
z(v)=\bar{v}
. Let
N_{v}=\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi;\langle\alpha,v\rangle>0}U_{\alpha}
. Set
M=M_{\bar{v}}={}^{z}M_{v}={}^{z}M_{b}
and
b_{M}=zb\sigma(z)^{-1}
. By
[
15
, Lemma 3.1]
,
b_{M}
is a lift of some element in
\Omega_{M}
, and is superbasic in
M({\breve{F}})
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
and
\alpha\in\Phi-\Phi_{M_{b}}
. Then
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha}=\emptyset
and

|R({\lambda})\cap(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cup\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha})|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}|\langle\beta,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|,

where the equality holds if and only if either
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

As
\mu
is minuscule, it follows from Proposition
2.9
and Lemma
2.7
(1) that
{\lambda}^{\natural}
is minuscule and that

(i)

\displaystyle{\lambda}_{\gamma^{-1}}-{\lambda}_{\gamma}=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle\in\{0,\pm 1\}\text{ for }\gamma\in\Phi.

By assumption, we have
\langle\alpha,v\rangle\neq 0
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha}=\emptyset
. By symmetry, we may assume
{\lambda}_{\alpha}\geqslant 0
and there exist integers

0=b_{0}\leqslant c_{1}<b_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant c_{r}<b_{r}=|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}|

such that for
1\leqslant k\leqslant r
we have

\displaystyle{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}<0\text{ for }b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k}\text{ and }{\lambda}_{\alpha^{j}}\geqslant 0\text{ for }c_{k}+1\leqslant j\leqslant b_{k}.

It follows from (i) that

(ii)

\displaystyle\text{ if }b_{k-1}<c_{k},\text{ then }{\lambda}_{\alpha^{b_{k-1}+1}}={\lambda}_{\alpha^{c_{k}}}=-1\text{ and }{\lambda}_{\alpha^{c_{k}+1}}={\lambda}_{\alpha^{b_{k-1}}}=0.

If
b_{k-1}<c_{k}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant r
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\ |R({\lambda})\cap\{\pm\alpha^{i};b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k}\}|

\displaystyle=|R({\lambda})\cap\{-\alpha^{i};b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k}\}|

\displaystyle=|\{b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k};{\lambda}_{-\alpha^{i-1}}\geqslant 0,{\lambda}_{-\alpha^{i}}-{\lambda}_{-\alpha^{i-1}}=1\}|

\displaystyle=|\{b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k};{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}\leqslant-1,{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}=1\}|

\displaystyle=|\{b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k};{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}=1\}|-1

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=b_{k-1}+1}^{c_{k}}|{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}|

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=b_{k-1}+1}^{c_{k}}|\langle\alpha^{i},{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|,

where the first equality follows from that
{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}<0
and hence
\alpha^{i}\notin R({\lambda})
for
b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k}
; the third one follows from that
{\lambda}_{-\gamma}=-1-{\lambda}_{\gamma}
for
\gamma\in\Phi
; the fourth one follows from that
{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}\leqslant-1
for
b_{k-1}+1\leqslant i\leqslant c_{k}
but
1+{\lambda}_{\alpha^{b_{k-1}+1}}={\lambda}_{\alpha^{b_{k-1}}}=0
by (ii); the fifth one follows from (i) and the equality
\sum_{i=b_{k-1}+1}^{c_{k}}{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}={\lambda}_{\alpha^{b_{k-1}}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{c_{k}}}=1
by (ii); the last one follows from (i).

Similarly, for
1\leqslant k\leqslant r
,

\displaystyle\quad\ |R({\lambda})\cap\{\pm\alpha^{i};c_{k}+1\leqslant i\leqslant b_{k}\}|

\displaystyle=|\{c_{k}+1\leqslant i\leqslant b_{k};{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}\geqslant 1,{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}=1\}|

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}|\{c_{k}+1\leqslant i\leqslant b_{k};{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}=1\}|,&\text{ if }b_{k-1}=c_{k};\\
|\{c_{k}+1\leqslant i\leqslant b_{k};{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}-{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i-1}}=1\}|-1,&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=c_{k}+1}^{b_{k}}|\langle\alpha^{i},{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|,&\text{ if }b_{k-1}=c_{k}\\
-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=c_{k}+1}^{b_{k}}|\langle\alpha^{i},{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

where the second equality follows from that
{\lambda}_{\alpha^{i}}\geqslant 0
for
c_{k}+1\leqslant i\leqslant b_{k}
and that
{\lambda}_{\alpha^{c_{k}}}\geqslant 0
if and only if
b_{k-1}=c_{k}
.

Therefore,

|R({\lambda})\cap(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cup\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha})|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}|\langle\beta,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|,

where the equality holds if and only if
b_{k-1}=c_{k}
for
1\leqslant k\leqslant r
, that is,
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. The proof is finished.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\alpha\in\Pi
(see §
1.2
). Then
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
is infinite if and only if
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}=\Pi
. Moreover, in this case,
M_{b}=G
.

Proof.

It follows from a case-by-case analysis on the Dynkin diagram of
\mathbb{S}_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

For
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
the map
\alpha\mapsto z(\alpha)
gives a bijection
R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}\cong R_{z({\lambda}^{\natural}),b_{M}}^{M}(z({\lambda}))
. As a consequence,
|R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}|\leqslant\dim X_{z({\lambda}^{\natural})}^{M}(b_{M})
. Here the subset
R_{z({\lambda}^{\natural}),b_{M}}^{M}(z({\lambda}))\subseteq\Phi_{M}
is defined in §
2.2
for
G=M
.

Proof.

Since
z(\Phi_{M_{b}}^{+})=\Phi_{M}^{+}
, we have
{\lambda}_{\alpha}=z({\lambda})_{z(\alpha)}
for
\alpha\in\Phi_{M_{v}}
. Hence the first statement follows. The second statement follows from Proposition
2.9
that
|R_{z({\lambda}^{\natural}),b_{M}}^{M}(z({\lambda}))|=\dim X_{z({\lambda}^{\natural})}^{z({\lambda}),M}(b_{M})
.
∎

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
. Then
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
if and only if (1)
z({\lambda})\in\mathcal{A}_{z({\lambda}^{\natural}),b_{M}}^{M,{\rm top}}
and (2) for each
\alpha\in\Phi-\Phi_{M_{b}}
, either
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

As
{\lambda}^{\natural}
is conjugate to
\mu
, we have

|\langle\rho,\mu\rangle|=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi_{M_{v}}^{+}\cup\Phi_{N_{v}}}|\langle\alpha,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\dim X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)

\displaystyle=|R({\lambda})|

\displaystyle=|R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}|+|R({\lambda})\cap(\Phi-\Phi_{M_{b}})|

\displaystyle=|R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}|+\sum_{\mathcal{O}}|R({\lambda})\cap\pm\mathcal{O}|

\displaystyle\leqslant|R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}|+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi_{N_{v}}}|\langle\alpha,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|R({\lambda})\cap\Phi_{M_{b}}|+\langle\rho,\overline{{\lambda}^{\natural}}\rangle-\langle\rho_{M},\overline{z({\lambda}^{\natural})}^{M}\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\dim X_{z({\lambda}^{\natural})}^{M}(b_{M})+\langle\rho,\mu\rangle-\langle\rho_{M},\overline{z({\lambda}^{\natural})}^{M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\dim X_{\mu}(b),

where
\mathcal{O}
ranges over
p(b\sigma)
-orbits of
\Phi_{N_{v}}
, and moreover, by Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
5.4
the equality holds if and only if the conditions (1) and (2) hold. The proof is finished.
∎

5.3.
The action of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
on
{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}(b)

Notice that
\mathbb{J}_{b}
is generated by
I\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
,
\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
and
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
for
\alpha\in\Pi
. In this subsection we study the action of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
on
{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}(b)
. Assume that
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
is finite and let
{\tilde{w}}
be the longest element of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\alpha,{\tilde{w}}
be as in §
5.3
. Then
\{{\lambda}_{\beta};\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\}=\{-{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})_{\beta};\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\}
for
{\lambda}\in Y
.

Proof.

Recall that
{\lambda}_{\beta}=-\tilde{\beta}({\lambda})
for
\beta\in\Phi
. The statement follows by noticing that
{\tilde{w}}
sends
\mathcal{O}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}}
to
-\mathcal{O}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\alpha,{\tilde{w}}
be as in §
5.3
. Let
{\lambda}\in Y
such that either
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. For
\gamma\in\Phi
with
{\lambda}_{\gamma}\geqslant 0
we have
{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})_{p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma)}\geqslant 0
. If, moreover,
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
, then
p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})=R({\tilde{w}}({\lambda}))
.

Proof.

We argue by contradiction. Set
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
and
\gamma^{\prime}=p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma)
. Suppose
{\lambda}_{\gamma}\geqslant 0
but
{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\gamma^{\prime}}<0
, that is,

(i)
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle\geqslant 0
, and
\gamma<0
if
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=0
;

(ii)
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle\leqslant 0
, and
\gamma^{\prime}>0
if
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=0
.

By assumption and Lemma
5.1
, we have

(iii)
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 1
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
if
\beta\in\Phi
is a sum of roots in
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
.

Case(1):
\langle\alpha^{d},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\neq-1
. Then
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
is an orthogonal set and
{\tilde{w}}=\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}s_{\tilde{\beta}}
. So
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})=p({\tilde{w}})({\lambda}-\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cap\Phi^{+}}\beta^{\vee})
and hence
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}-\sum_{\beta\in E\cap\Phi^{+}}\beta^{\vee}\rangle
, where
E=\{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha};\langle\gamma,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0\}
. If
E\subseteq\Phi^{-}
, then
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle
. By (i) and (ii) this implies that
\gamma^{\prime}>0
,
\gamma<0
and
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=0
. As
E
consists of minus simple roots and
\gamma^{\prime}=p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma)=(\prod_{\beta\in E}s_{\beta})(\gamma)
, we deduce that
\gamma
is a sum of roots in
E
, contradicting (iii) since
{\lambda}_{\gamma}=0
. Thus
E
contains a unique highest root
\theta
of
\Phi^{+}
and
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}-\theta^{\vee}\rangle
. By (i), (ii) and that
\langle\gamma,\theta^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0
, we have
\langle\gamma,\theta^{\vee}\rangle\geqslant 1
. If
\gamma=\theta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, then
\gamma^{\prime}=-\theta<0
(since
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
is orthogonal). As
{\lambda}_{\gamma}\geqslant 0
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, by (iii) we have
\langle{\lambda},\gamma\rangle={\lambda}_{\gamma}+1\geqslant 2
. So
{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\gamma^{\prime}}=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle-2\geqslant 2-2=0
, which is a contradiction. So
\gamma\neq\pm\theta
and hence
\langle\gamma,\theta^{\vee}\rangle=1
(since
\theta
is a long root). By (i) and (ii) we have
0\leqslant\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle\leqslant 1
. If
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=1
, then
\gamma^{\prime}=(\prod_{\beta\in E-\{\theta\}}s_{\beta})(\gamma-\theta)>0
by (ii). As
\gamma-\theta\in\Phi^{-}
,
\gamma-\theta
is a sum of roots in
E-\{\theta\}
, contradicting that
\mathcal{O}
is strongly orthogonal by Lemma
5.1
(2). So
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=0
and hence
\gamma<0
by (i). In particular,
\langle\gamma,\theta^{\vee}\rangle\leqslant 0
as
\theta^{\vee}
is dominant, which contradicts that
\langle\gamma,\theta^{\vee}\rangle=1
.

Case(2):
\langle\alpha^{d},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=-1
. Let
\xi=\alpha+\alpha^{d}
. Then
|\mathcal{O}_{\xi}|=d
and
{\tilde{w}}=\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\xi}}s_{\tilde{\beta}}
by Lemma
5.1
. So
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})=p({\tilde{w}})({\lambda}-\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\xi}\cap\Phi^{+}}\beta^{\vee})
and hence
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}-\sum_{\beta\in E\cap\Phi^{+}}\beta^{\vee}\rangle
, where
E=\{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\xi};\langle\gamma,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0\}
. Notice that
E
consists of at most one element. If
E=\emptyset
, then
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}
and
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle
, contradicting (i) and (ii). So
E=\{\xi^{i_{0}}\}
for some
1\leqslant i_{0}\leqslant d
. If
\xi^{i_{0}}<0
, then
\alpha^{i_{0}},\alpha^{i_{0}+d}
are both minus simple roots and
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle
. By (i) and (ii) we have
\gamma^{\prime}>0
,
\gamma<0
and
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=0
. As
\gamma^{\prime}=s_{\xi^{i_{0}}}(\gamma)=s_{\alpha^{i_{0}}}s_{\alpha^{i_{0}+d}}s_{\alpha^{i_{0}}}(\gamma)
, we deduce that
\gamma
is a sum of roots in
\{\alpha^{i_{0}},\alpha^{i_{0}+d}\}
, contradicting (iii). So
\xi^{i_{0}}>0
and
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}-(\xi^{i_{0}})^{\vee}\rangle
. Moreover, as
\xi^{i_{0}}=\alpha^{i_{0}}+\alpha^{i_{0}+d}
, exactly one of
\{\alpha^{i_{0}},\alpha^{i_{0}+d}\}
is a positive highest root. By symmetry, we can assume
\alpha^{i_{0}}<0
and
\alpha^{i_{0}+d}>0
. By (ii) and that
\langle\gamma,(\xi^{i_{0}})^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0
we have
\langle\gamma,(\xi^{i_{0}})^{\vee}\rangle\geqslant 1
. If
\gamma=\xi^{i_{0}}
, then
\gamma^{\prime}=-\xi^{i_{0}}<0
. By (iii) we have
\langle{\lambda},\gamma\rangle={\lambda}_{\gamma}+1\geqslant 2
and hence
{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\gamma^{\prime}}=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle-2\geqslant 2-2=0
, which is a contradiction. So
\gamma\neq\pm\xi^{i_{0}}
and
\langle\gamma,(\xi^{i_{0}})^{\vee}\rangle=1
(since
\xi^{i_{0}}
is a long root), which means
\gamma^{\prime}=s_{\xi^{i_{0}}}(\gamma)=\gamma-\xi^{i_{0}}
. By (i) and (ii) we have
0\leqslant\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle\leqslant 1
. If
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=1
, then
\langle\gamma^{\prime},{\lambda}^{\prime}\rangle=0
and hence
0<\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma-\xi^{i_{0}}=(\gamma-\alpha^{i_{0}+d})-\alpha^{i_{0}}\leqslant-\alpha^{i_{0}}
by (ii), where the last inequality follows from that
\alpha^{i_{0}+d}
is a positive highest root. As
-\alpha_{i_{0}}
is a simple root, we deduce that
\gamma^{\prime}=-\alpha^{i_{0}}
and hence
\gamma=\alpha^{i_{0}+d}\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, contradicting (iii) since
{\lambda}_{\gamma}=0
. So
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}\rangle=0
and hence
\gamma<0
by (i), which together with the equality
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma-\xi^{i_{0}}\in\Phi
implies that
0\leqslant\gamma^{\prime}+\alpha^{i_{0}+d}=\gamma-\alpha^{i_{0}}<-\alpha^{i_{0}}
. So
\gamma-\alpha^{i_{0}}=0
, that is,
\gamma=\alpha^{i_{0}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, which contradicts (iii) since
{\lambda}_{\gamma}=0
. The first statement is proved.

Let
\gamma\in R({\lambda})
, that is,
\langle\gamma,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle=-1
and
{\lambda}_{\gamma^{-1}}\geqslant 0
. By Lemma
2.7
and the first statement of the lemma we have

\langle p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma),{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})^{\natural}\rangle=\langle p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma),p({\tilde{w}})({\lambda}^{\natural})\rangle=\langle\gamma,{\lambda}^{\natural}\rangle=-1

and
{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})_{p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma)^{-1}}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})_{p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma^{-1})}\geqslant 0
, that is,
p({\tilde{w}})(\gamma)\in R({\tilde{w}}({\lambda}))
and hence
p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})\subseteq R({\tilde{w}}({\lambda}))
. By symmetry (see Lemma
5.6
), we have
p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})=R({\tilde{w}}({\lambda}))
. The second statement follows.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\alpha,{\tilde{w}}
be as in §
5.3
. For
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
we have

(1) either
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
;

(2) if
{\lambda}\neq{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
, then either
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
;

(3) if
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}({\lambda})\cap\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
, then
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\lambda}
or
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from Lemma
5.3
and Corollary
5.5
(2).

Suppose
{\lambda}\neq{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
. By Lemma
5.6
we have

\{{\lambda}_{\beta};\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\}=\{-{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})_{\beta};\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\},

which, together with (1), implies that either
{\lambda}_{\beta}=0
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\geqslant 1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
or
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. By Lemma
1.3
(2), the first case implies that
{\lambda}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
, contradicting our assumption. So the statement (2) follows.

Suppose
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}({\lambda})\cap\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
. By (1) and the equality
\chi_{\beta}=-\tilde{\beta}(\chi)
for
\beta\in\Phi
and
\chi\in Y
, we see that
{\lambda}
and
{\lambda}^{\prime}
are contained in the union of

\{y\in Y_{\mathbb{R}};\tilde{\beta}(y)\leqslant 0,\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\}\text{ and }\{y\in Y_{\mathbb{R}};\tilde{\beta}(y)>0,\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\},

which are the closed anti-dominant Weyl chamber and the open dominant Weyl chamber for
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
respectively. Therefore, as
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}({\lambda})
, we see that
{\lambda}={\lambda}^{\prime}
if they are both dominant or both anti-dominant for
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
, and
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
otherwise. The statement (3) is proved.
∎

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\alpha,{\tilde{w}}
be as in §
5.3
. For
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
we have

(1)
{\tilde{w}}\overline{X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)}=\overline{X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)}
if
{\lambda}_{\beta}=0
for some
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
;

(2)
{\tilde{w}}X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)\subseteq X_{\mu}^{{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})}(b)
if
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Let
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
such that
{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{{\lambda}^{\prime}}}
intersects
{\rm Irr}({\tilde{w}}\overline{X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)})
. By Lemma
1.1
,
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}({\lambda})
. Thus
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\lambda}
or
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
by Lemma
5.8
(3). If
{\lambda}_{\beta}=0
for some
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, then
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\lambda}
by Lemma
5.8
(2). So the statement (1) follows.

Suppose
{\lambda}_{\beta}\leqslant-1
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. Then
s_{\tilde{\beta}}t^{\lambda}>t^{\lambda}
for
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. Thus
\ell({\tilde{w}}t^{\lambda})=\ell({\tilde{w}})+\ell(t^{\lambda})
and

{\tilde{w}}It^{\lambda}K\subseteq I{\tilde{w}}t^{\lambda}K=It^{{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})}K.

So
{\tilde{w}}X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)\subseteq X_{\mu}^{{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})}(b)
and the statement (2) follows.
∎

5.4.
Equivalence relation on
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v)

For
{\lambda},{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
, we write
{\lambda}\sim{\lambda}^{\prime}
if
\mathbb{J}_{b}{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)}=\mathbb{J}_{b}{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{{\lambda}^{\prime}}(b)}
. Notice that
\mathbb{J}_{b}{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)
is a single
\mathbb{J}_{b}
-orbit of
{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}(b)
by Proposition
2.9
.

Let
v\in V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}\cap Y
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v)
(resp.
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}(v)
) denote the set of
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
(resp.
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}
) such that
{\lambda}_{\alpha}\geqslant 0
for
\alpha\in\Phi_{N_{v}}
. Here
\Phi_{N_{v}}=\{\alpha\in\Phi;\langle\alpha,v\rangle>0\}
is the set of roots in
N_{v}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
. Then
{\lambda}\sim\chi
for some
\chi\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v)
.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Lemma
1.1
,
t^{n\delta}It^{\lambda}K\subseteq\cup_{x\leq t^{\lambda}}It^{nv}xK
. Let
\chi_{x,n}\in Y
such that
It^{nv}xK=It^{\chi_{x,n}}K
for
x\leq t^{\lambda}
. Then
t^{nv}{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)\subseteq\cup_{x\leq t^{\lambda}}{\rm Irr}\overline{X_{\mu}^{\chi_{x,n}}(b)}
. Thus for sufficiently large
n
we have
(\chi_{x,n})_{\alpha}\geqslant 0
for
x\leq t^{\lambda}
and
\alpha\in\Phi_{N_{v}}
. So the statement follows.
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
{\lambda},{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
such that
{\lambda}\sim{\lambda}^{\prime}
. Then there exists a sequence
{\lambda}={\lambda}_{0},{\lambda}_{1},\dots,{\lambda}_{r}={\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
such that
{\lambda}_{i}={\tilde{w}}_{i}({\lambda}_{i-1})
and
R({\lambda}_{i})=p({\tilde{w}}_{i})R({\lambda}_{i-1})
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
, where
{\tilde{w}}_{i}\in\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
or
{\tilde{w}}_{i}
is the longest element of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{i}}}
for some
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi
.

Proof.

By assumption, there exist
C\in{\rm Irr}X_{\mu}^{\lambda}(b)
and
g\in\mathbb{J}_{b}
such that
gC\subseteq\overline{X_{\mu}^{{\lambda}^{\prime}}(b)}
. Since
b\in\Omega
,
\mathbb{J}_{b}
is generated by
I\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
,
\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
and
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
for
\alpha\in\Pi
such that
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}
is finite. We may assume
g
lies in one of the sets
I\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
,
\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
and
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
. If
g\in I\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
, then
{\lambda}={\lambda}^{\prime}
and there is nothing to prove. If
g=\omega
for some
\omega\in\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
, then
\omega It^{\lambda}K/K=It^{\omega({\lambda})}K/K
. Hence
{\lambda}^{\prime}=\omega({\lambda})
and
R({\lambda}^{\prime})=p(\omega)R({\lambda})
by Lemma
2.8
. Suppose
g\in W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}=\{1,{\tilde{w}}\}
, where
{\tilde{w}}
is the unique longest element of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
. Then
{\lambda}^{\prime}
equals
{\lambda}
or
{\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
by Lemma
5.8
(3). So we can assume that
{\lambda}\neq{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
and it remains to show
R({\lambda}^{\prime})=p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})
. The statement follows from Lemma
5.8
(2) and Lemma
5.7
.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

We have
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}=\cup_{v^{\prime}\in p(\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})(v)}\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
. By Lemma
5.10
, there exist
v^{\prime}\in p(\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b})(v)
and
\chi\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v^{\prime})
such that
{\lambda}\sim\chi
. By Lemma
5.11
, we can assume that
\chi\neq{\lambda}={\tilde{w}}(\chi)\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}
, where (1)
{\tilde{w}}\in\Omega\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
or (2)
{\tilde{w}}
is as in §
5.3
. It suffices to show
{\lambda}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(p({\tilde{w}})(v^{\prime}))
, that is,
{\lambda}_{p({\tilde{w}})(\beta)}={\tilde{w}}(\chi)_{p({\tilde{w}})(\beta)}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\Phi_{N_{v^{\prime}}}
. Notice that
\chi_{\beta}\geqslant 0
for
\beta\in\Phi_{N_{v^{\prime}}}
. Then the case (1) follows from Lemma
1.3
(3), and the case (2) follows from Lemma
5.8
(2) and Lemma
5.7
as desired.
∎

5.5.
The action of
\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
on
V^{b\sigma}

Notice that
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
preserves the affine space
V^{b\sigma}=\{v\in V;b\sigma(v)=v\}
. Via the restriction to
V^{b\sigma}
we can identify
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
with an affine reflection group of
V^{b\sigma}
, whose affine root hyperplanes are
H_{a}\cap V^{b\sigma}
for
a\in\tilde{\Phi}^{+}
with
V^{b\sigma}\neq H_{a}\cap V^{b\sigma}\neq\emptyset
. Moreover,
\Delta\cap V^{b\sigma}
is an alcove for
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
, with respect to which the simple affine reflections are the longest elements of
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
for
\alpha\in\Pi
with
W_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\alpha}}}
finite. We fix a special point
e^{\prime}
in the closure of
\Delta\cap V^{b\sigma}
for
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
.

We recall a lemma on root systems.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
E
be some euclidean space and let
\Sigma\subseteq E
be a root system. Let
v_{1},v_{2}\in E
be two regular points for
\Sigma
(that is, not contained in any root hyperplane of
\Sigma
). Then there exists root hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{r}
separating
v_{1}
from
v_{2}
such that
s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(v_{1})
and
v_{2}
are in the same Weyl chamber.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
e^{\prime}
be as in §
5.5
. Let
v\in V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}
. Then for
{\tilde{w}}\in\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
there exist affine root hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{r}
of
V^{b\sigma}
passing through
e^{\prime}
such that

(1)
H_{i}
separates
e^{\prime}+v
from
e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v)
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
;

(2)
e^{\prime}+v=s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v))
.

Moreover,
s_{H_{i}}=\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha_{i}}}s_{\tilde{\beta}}
for some
\alpha_{i}\in\Phi
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha_{i}}
is strongly orthogonal.

Proof.

First note that
V^{p(b\sigma)}
is the underlining vector space of the affine space
V^{b\sigma}
. As
p({\tilde{w}})
preserves
V^{p(b\sigma)}
, we see that
v,p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v)\in V^{p(b\sigma)}_{\rm gen}
. Hence
e^{\prime}+v,e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})(v)
are regular points for the root system associated to
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
with origin
e^{\prime}
. By Lemma
5.13
, there are affine root hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{r}
of
V^{b\sigma}
(passing through
e^{\prime}
) separating
e^{\prime}+v
from
e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v)
such that
s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v))
and
e^{\prime}+v
are contained in the same Weyl chamber of
V^{b\sigma}
with origin
e^{\prime}
. Suppose
e^{\prime}+v\neq s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v))
, that is,
v\neq p(s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}{\tilde{w}}^{-1})(v)\in W_{0}(v)
. Then there exists
\alpha\in\Phi
whose root hyperplane
H_{\alpha}
separates
v
from
p(s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}{\tilde{w}}^{-1})(v)
. In particular,
H_{\alpha}\cap V^{b\sigma}
is an affine root hyperplane for
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
. As
e^{\prime}
is a special point for
W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
, there exists some affine root hyperplane
H
of
V^{b\sigma}
passing through
e^{\prime}
which is parallel to
H_{\alpha}\cap V^{b\sigma}
. Then
H
separates
e^{\prime}+v
from
s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v))
, contradicting our assumption. So we have
e^{\prime}+v=s_{H_{1}}\cdots s_{H_{r}}(e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v))
as desired.

Now we show the “Moreover” part. By Lemma
5.1
, there exists
a_{i}=(\alpha_{i},k_{i})\in\tilde{\Phi}^{+}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha_{i}}
is strongly orthogonal and
s_{H_{i}}=\prod_{a\in\mathcal{O}_{a_{i}}}s_{a}
by viewing
s_{H_{i}}
as an element of
\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
. Notice that
e^{\prime}\in H_{i}=H_{a_{i}}\cap V^{b\sigma}
, that is,
a_{i}(e^{\prime})=-\langle\alpha_{i},e^{\prime}\rangle+k_{i}=0
. As
e^{\prime}
lies in the closure of
\Delta
, we have
|\langle\alpha_{i},e^{\prime}\rangle|\leqslant 1
, which together with the inclusion
a_{i}\in\tilde{\Phi}^{+}
implies that either
\alpha_{i}>0
and
k_{i}=1
or
\alpha_{i}<0
and
k_{i}=0
. In either case,
a_{i}={\tilde{\alpha}}_{i}
and the proof is finished.
∎

5.6.
Characterization of the equivalence relation

Let
v
,
z
,
M
,
b_{M}
be as in §
5.2
. We give an explicit description of the equivalence relation
\sim
on
\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v)
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
{\lambda},{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}^{\rm top}(v)
such that
{\lambda}\sim{\lambda}^{\prime}
. Then
{\lambda}^{\prime}=y({\lambda})
for some
y\in\Omega_{M_{b}}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
.

Proof.

First note that it suffices to find an element
y\in\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
such that
p(y)(v)=v
and
{\lambda}^{\prime}=y({\lambda})
. Indeed, the conditions
p(y)(v)=v
and
y\in\mathbb{J}_{b}
imply that
y\in\tilde{W}_{M_{b}}
and
y(V^{b\sigma})=V^{b\sigma}
. Noticing that
V^{b\sigma}\subseteq\Delta_{M_{b}}
(since
V^{b\sigma}\cap\Delta\neq\emptyset
), we have
y(\Delta_{M_{b}})=\Delta_{M_{b}}
and hence
y\in\Omega_{M_{b}}
.

By Lemma
5.11
, there is
{\tilde{w}}\in\tilde{W}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
such that
{\lambda}^{\prime}={\tilde{w}}({\lambda})
and
R({\lambda}^{\prime})=p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})
. If
p({\tilde{w}})(v)=v
, the statement follows as in the above paragraph. Suppose
p({\tilde{w}})(v)\neq v
. Let
e^{\prime}
,
H_{i}
and
\alpha_{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
be as in Lemma
5.14
. We construct
x_{i}\in W^{a}\cap\mathbb{J}_{b}
such that
p(x_{i})=p(s_{H_{i}})
and
x_{i}({\lambda})={\lambda}
as follows.

As
H_{i}
separates
e^{\prime}+v
from
e^{\prime}+p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v)
, without loss of generality we may assume that

\langle\alpha_{i},v\rangle<0<\langle\alpha_{i},p({\tilde{w}})^{-1}(v)\rangle=\langle p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha_{i}),v\rangle.

Let
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha_{i}}
. As
v\in V^{p(b\sigma)}
and that
p({\tilde{w}})
commutes with
p(b\sigma)
, we have

\langle p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha),v\rangle=\langle p({\tilde{w}})(-\alpha_{i}),v\rangle<0.

So
-p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha)\in\Phi_{N_{v}}
. Moreover, as
{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mu,b}(v)
, we have
{\lambda}_{-p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha)}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
and

{\lambda}_{p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha)}^{\prime}=-{\lambda}_{-p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha)}^{\prime}-1\leqslant-1.

By definition,
p({\tilde{w}})(\alpha)\notin R({\lambda}^{\prime})
and hence
R({\lambda}^{\prime})\cap p({\tilde{w}})\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha_{i}}=\emptyset
. Therefore,
R({\lambda})\cap\mathcal{O}_{-\alpha_{i}}=\emptyset
as
R({\lambda}^{\prime})=p({\tilde{w}})R({\lambda})
.

We claim that
{\lambda}_{\beta}
